On every test case the output is bit-for-bit identical to the original (same result hash), including the default run, 21 steps, 0 steps, odd shapes like 37×1×5, 1×3×4 and 50×3×300, and a run with 7 threads. The executable is still `cahn_hilliard`, only the two existing files changed, and the only new requirement is OpenMP itself.\n\n**Performance on this machine (2× EPYC 7763, 256 hardware threads):**\n\n| Grid | Original | Parallel |\n|---|---|---|\n| 64³ (default) | ~165 MCUPS | ~5–7 GCUPS |\n| 128³ | ~165 MCUPS | ~15–16 GCUPS |\n| 256³ | ~168 MCUPS | ~21–22 GCUPS |\n| 512³ | — | ~4 GCUPS |\n\nThe 512³ grid stops scaling at about 4 GCUPS from 64 threads up, because it is limited by memory bandwidth. Each slice of that grid is too large to stay in cache, so the fused step below can't help there. Timings were noisy from run to run (the 64³ case ranged from about 2.6 to 7 GCUPS).\n\n**`CMakeLists.txt`:** it now requires OpenMP (`find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`) and links it.\n\n**`cahn_hilliard.cpp`:**\n- **One parallel region for the whole time loop:** threads are started once rather than at every step. Each thread gets a fixed block of grid rows.\n- **Fused time step:** each thread first computes μ (the chemical potential) on the edge rows its neighbours need, then waits once for all threads. It then works through its own rows, computing μ one slice ahead of the update, so μ is reused from cache instead of being written out and read back. That leaves two synchronisation points per step.\n- **Memory placement:** the arrays are no longer zero-filled serially. Each thread initialises its own block, so memory is spread across the two sockets.\n- **Thread pinning in code:** without pinning, the 64³ case was very slow with 256 threads (about 50–200 MCUPS). OpenMP's `proc_bind` setting alone did nothing unless `OMP_PLACES` was set, so the program now pins threads itself with `sched_setaffinity`, spread evenly across sockets and cores. If you set `OMP_PROC_BIND` or `OMP_PLACES` yourself, it leaves pinning to OpenMP.\n- **Faster inner loop:** the boundary cells at each end of a row are handled separately, so the main part of the row runs as a vectorised loop. The arithmetic order is unchanged, which is why the results still match exactly.\n- **Validation:** the NaN/Inf and min/max checks now run in parallel. The `-r` output copies the result into a standard vector before printing, outside the timed section."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":516,"cache_read_input_tokens":38523,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":516},"output_tokens":6,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"264159de-32a5-4174-a761-650670cec1be","uuid":"36245bcd-a6d5-4434-80cd-424565083e66","timestamp":"2026-10-05T16:46:59.798Z","request_id":"req_011CfjRtmecBiSKRJZrGhSyN"}
